## Round 3 — design clarification after scout, before implementation
- The initial phrase “valid ledger for existing seeded conflicts” cannot honestly mean one two-sided record for every violation: `relations[1]` points to absent `missing-map` and has no second cited claim. Therefore preserve that third checker violation outside the two-sided ledger. Ledger two entries refer to the two timeline conflicts (`arrival` versus `false-sighting`, `arrival.depends_on` versus `later.tick`); each side is an explicit timeline event ID and assertion field, resolving to the event's own in-world `source_id`. `why` is required editorial text but not semantically proven. This narrows the claim rather than fabricating a denial.
- Choose a universal required `contradictions.json` fifth section for the four example bibles, using `[]` where no pair is asserted; omission should remain an error like every other required table. References use `{event_id, field}` with a documented allowed set (`place_id`, `tick`, `depends_on`), no copied source or prose. Reject missing event IDs, invalid fields, identical claim references, malformed records, and references whose event source is unresolved; never infer contradiction from fields or silently resolve conflict. No arbitrary optional-file fallback.

## Round 4 — scope clarification after first-wave critique
- “Name collision” is an editorial review diagnostic for **exactly identical display strings among unambiguous distinct entity IDs**, not proof of shared identity, source falsity, or impossible homonymy. No exception field this round: even legitimate homonyms are surfaced for human disambiguation rather than silently merged or rejected as canon. Skip rows whose ID is duplicated (existing duplicate-ID violation remains) and schema-invalid rows. Compare raw case-sensitive names; whitespace/case/Unicode variants are not normalized. Both source-cited first-appearance rows are retained.

## Round 6 — design clarification after scouts, before implementation
- Use optional `provenance` list **on an item entity**: absence means no provenance asserted, not an error. First entry is a cited acquisition `{tick, from_holder: null, to_holder, source_id}`; later entries are cited transfers with nonnull `from_holder`, all holder IDs resolvable and unambiguous. Require strictly increasing integer ticks within that item's listed history; equal/reversed ticks are reported as ambiguous/order violation, never sorted or silently interpreted. A transfer's `from_holder` must equal immediately preceding record's `to_holder`; report break locally but retain both records. No transfer inference from `carries`, timeline prose, or gameplay, and transfer ticks are local order markers not automatically linked to timeline events. This is one item-chain consistency rule plus shape validation, not a general provenance ontology.
- Scope boundary: optional list only on item entities; invalid present value is reported, not treated as absent. Duplicate item/holder IDs are already diagnosed and do not qualify as unambiguous chain evidence. Source IDs resolve to in-world records, not proof of textual support or canon truth.

No source row deleted, renamed, selected, or merged.
